Experimental — still being tested

PGO Forecast Lab

Weekly game forecasts

See who the model favors, its estimated scores, and how the predictions compare with final results.

Each prediction becomes final 60 minutes before kickoff. We keep the last saved prediction before that deadline and record its misses as well as its wins.

A draft can change before its own deadline. A locked prediction cannot. An early game does not lock every other game that week.

Weekly predictions

Forecast snapshot created: September 8, 2026 at 11:18 AM EDT. Injuries beyond the quarterback are not included. These forecasts assume the listed quarterback plays. Team injury information is below.

“SEA by 3.0 points” means the model favors Seattle by three. Combined points adds both teams’ estimates.

Estimated scores show averages to one decimal, not a literal final score. Very close scores can still look equal after rounding; the favored team comes from the original unrounded difference. Scores and combined points are rounded separately, so the displayed sum can differ by 0.1.

How much should you trust the scores? In historical testing, the combined-score estimate missed by about 11 points per game on average. It did not clearly beat using a simple league average. Treat these scores as an experiment.

How we track every forecast — saved versions and technical details

Research status: EXPERIMENTAL / HOLD. Estimated scores and combined points are shown to one decimal. The favored team uses the original unrounded margin, including edges below 0.1 point. Evaluation uses unrounded values.

Historical totals test and limitations.

  1. Review sources: verify roster, expected starters, injury coverage, and source dates. Missing formal reports remain unknown; refreshes require review.
  2. Save a revision: record a dated forecast revision before the relevant game's deadline. Keep every earlier revision.
  3. Lock each matchup: at T-60, freeze that game's scores, spread, and total together from its last eligible saved revision.
  4. Grade every issued matchup: add verified final results, including losses and misses. Report the number graded and every outstanding game.
  5. Compare benchmarks: evaluate the same games against the stated baselines. Interim results do not satisfy a full-season acceptance gate.
  6. Test future versions: freeze a separate evaluation before future games. Research findings can propose a new version; they never rewrite issued predictions.

Saved revisions

  • Saved September 7, 2026 at 6:34 PM EDT; September 7 preseason; forecast revision
  • Saved September 8, 2026 at 11:23 AM EDT; PGO Corrected — Sep 8; forecast revision

Revisions are saved separately and cannot be submitted at or after the cutoff. A saved timestamp records local registration; the repository history records publication. A schedule change requires review and cannot silently extend an existing deadline.

Fresh weekly inputs require a separately reviewed source snapshot. Source refresh is not automated. Future weeks without a weekly edition remain available in the preseason baseline below.

Weekly results provenance

  • No weekly results recorded.

PGO Corrected — September 8, 2026

Experimental — accuracy is still being tested. “Corrected” means we repaired problems in the calculation. It does not mean this version has proved more accurate.

Higher ratings mean the model expects a stronger team; zero is the average of these 32 teams. A +5 rating does not mean a team should be favored by five points.

Roster information saved through September 8, 2026 at 11:01 AM EDT. Game and player performance comes from the 2025 regular season and earlier.

Injuries beyond the quarterback are not included. These ratings assume the listed quarterback plays. Being on the active roster does not mean a player is healthy. Reports need another review before each game locks.

Why does New England rank #1? The biggest boosts in this calculation come from recent game results, the quarterback’s passing numbers, the team’s passing numbers. Several performance inputs describe the same games, so they are not separate proof of the team’s strength. We have not established that this is the right ranking.

Choose a team below for its main reasons and injury information. New England · Jacksonville

How the model works — technical notes and sources

EXPERIMENTAL / HOLD. Model construction: September 8, 2026. Snapshot generated September 8, 2026 at 11:18 AM EDT. Outputs are model units, not established neutral-field point prices or rank-confidence intervals.

This version matches historical and current ACT eligibility, removes six roster/coaching transition inputs, uses statistic-specific QB exposure and enforces symmetric neutral-field predictions. It keeps four-game team history and a one-year QB half-life. Those construction repairs do not establish superior forecasting accuracy. The same historical seasons have already been examined.

The results-history input responds to winning or losing margins that beat the model’s own expectation, not fans’ or media expectations.

Fixed construction and evaluation contract · All inputs and source evidence

Model versions and snapshot dates

July 21 — archived PGO v1 ratings: the original saved preseason board. September 7 — refreshed v1 construction: active expected starters with the recovered original fit. September 8 — PGO Corrected: the separately fitted construction described below, used by this opening-week experiment.

The July/August frozen 272-game season forecast is a separate 75% v0 / 25% challenger blend. It is preserved as its own forecast record, not identified as the July v1 ratings board.

The earlier research name PGO v2 identifies a separate roster-age and draft-pedigree experiment that remained HOLD. It is not the name of this corrected edition. A model version describes the calculation; a snapshot date records a particular set of inputs and outputs. Every saved edition remains available. A newer date does not establish greater predictive accuracy.

                                                              How injuries could change the ratings

                                                              These estimates cover players other than quarterbacks. The original ratings and game predictions stay unchanged.

                                                              A negative change means a lower team rating. “Incomplete” means we lack a full injury report or cannot estimate every listed player. We show the change for players we can estimate, but no full adjusted rating. “Unknown” never means zero impact.

                                                              How we estimate injury effects — players, sources and limits

                                                              Research status: EXPERIMENTAL / HOLD. Base edition: pgo-corrected-week1-2026-09-08. Known-out status includes OUT, IR and PUP. Information must be saved before each game’s deadline, 60 minutes before kickoff. Complete covers the listed reports, not a guarantee that every absence is known.

                                                              Role is the median of the last four positive regular-season unit snap shares through 2025. A transferred or returning player’s historical usage is a proxy, not a current depth-chart claim. The fitted unit-role effect is an aggregate conditional association, not an individual star valuation or a measurement of replacement quality. QB and special teams are excluded. Past team performance may already reflect absences, so residual overlap remains possible. This role/status policy is unvalidated; scenario endpoints and their midpoint are not forecasts or calibrated probabilities.

                                                              Why teams rank here · All 32 September ratings

                                                              Open a team to see what raises and lowers its saved September 7 output. Higher totals rank higher; the gaps show how close neighboring teams are.

                                                              New England · Jacksonville · Read the outlier audit

                                                              How the contribution tables are calculated · Glossary and limits

                                                              Every summary shows the same seven input groups in the same order, so you can compare teams row by row. All are centered against the 32-team average and sum to the rating before rounding. These are fitted adjustments, not independent football grades, player values, or calibrated point-spread prices.

                                                              Team passing efficiency is shown separately from the other nine team-efficiency inputs. QB history combines eight QB inputs; roster composition combines returning offensive and defensive snap shares, incoming snap share, and rookie draft capital; coaching combines continuity and tenure. Other adjustments include availability, the QB lineup adjustment, venue, rest, and missing-data indicators. A zero here does not establish comprehensive injury coverage. Open the full breakdown to see every term.

                                                              EPA means expected points added. Team efficiency uses games through 2025 with a four-game half-life; QB efficiency uses shrunk player history. The PGO v0 input carries the earlier results rating forward without a new offseason shrink, unlike the separate v0 game-forecast baseline.

                                                              Audit finding: lower returning offensive snap share raises this saved model's output, and head-coach continuity lowers it. Later research improved when roster-continuity inputs were removed. The new input audit also identified different historical/current roster eligibility and preseason transition effects. These findings weaken a football interpretation of those contributions. Returning snap share tracks historical snap weight and a player's last recorded team, not the percentage of last season's team snaps retained. Issued values remain archived; research corrections are separate.

                                                              #1 NE · +7.219

                                                              Issued September 7 snapshot. Largest upward groups: Results history (+3.864), Team passing efficiency (+2.084). Largest downward group: Coaching (-0.672).

                                                              Drake Maye is the saved QB1; his QB passing term contributes +0.692 within the QB-history group.

                                                              Why first place is uncertain: recent results and passing drive NE high, but these inputs overlap. Its narrow lead also includes a counterintuitive roster-continuity benefit. The new input audit tests roster eligibility, preseason transition effects, and QB valuations. NE ranks first through fourth across seven specified constructions and stays first after removing the transition fields; none of the six new candidates clears the improvement screen. This saved rank does not establish that New England is clearly the best team in football.

                                                              September method, sources, and downloads

                                                              Generated September 7, 2026 at 10:01 PM UTC. This inference-policy change and the simple projected-score method remain experimental; through-2025 performance does not validate them.

                                                              • Roster policy: Only ACT roster entries; the latest verified active QB1 is selected by depth order. The same roster and QB assumptions apply to every week.
                                                              • Injury coverage: ACT is administrative status, not proof of health or game-day availability. No comprehensive practice, injury, or game-day inactive adjustment is included.
                                                              • History: Team and QB performance through the 2025 regular season. The historical fit and selected parameters are fixed. The embedded results-rating offseason convention is preserved.
                                                              • Projected totals: Total = (home 2025 points for/game + home points allowed/game + away points for/game + away points allowed/game) / 2. Expected scores = (total ± home margin) / 2. Whole scores are presentation rounding.
                                                              • Fit recovery: The exact July public fit was reconstructed and reproduced every saved field for all 32 teams. This active-roster / expected-starter inference policy is a separate, unvalidated candidate.
                                                              • Evaluation boundary: Primary: margin MAE. Also track margin RMSE, total MAE, per-team score MAE and winner accuracy. No calibrated win probabilities or automatic promotion.
                                                              • Schedule: Week 1 was independently checked against NFL.com. Later kickoffs are provider-listed and may change; Week 18 times are provisional.

                                                              Method, metrics, and scientific boundary

                                                              These are model-estimated home-score margins, not market prices, final-score predictions, or calibrated probabilities. MAE is mean absolute margin error; RMSE gives larger misses more weight.

                                                              Winner accuracy is derived from frozen margin signs. It excludes actual ties and zero-margin abstentions, reports its denominator, and is separate from calibration and every promotion gate. The theoretical 50% benchmark is a reference; no observed coin-flip record is invented.

                                                              Constant zero and venue-only (+2.5 Home, 0 Neutral) are fixed diagnostic margin baselines outside the preregistered full-season gate. Market and McCabe game-pick comparisons are unavailable because they were not captured before kickoff.

                                                              Raw source cutoff: 2026-07-21T12:00:00-04:00. Raw attestation time: 2026-08-26T16:07:24-04:00.

                                                              Partial metrics are descriptive only. The unchanged canonical grade requires all 272 exact final results and is the only path to a prospective PASS, HOLD, or BLOCKED receipt.
